# MenuList Release-Candidate Certification

**Status:** CERTIFICATION BLOCKED

**Candidate branch:** `staging`

**Initial candidate commit:** `b857a164944012d42131917e7c62215c94022c0f`

**Current local repository base commit:** `4586bb8523a92b1c7d0ea37a5b17267432346cf6`

**Current tested MenuList filesystem:** the coordinated filesystem that was
locally validated on base `59e1dc2561750ed2d97764fa30df7614c3071fd2`
was committed in one consolidated release as `4586bb8523a92b1c7d0ea37a5b17267432346cf6`.
It includes MLRC-104 through MLRC-113, including registry/inventory evidence,
scheduler-cost correction, owner accessibility corrections, interactive-control
coverage, Projects action-menu keyboard support, and reachable `src/modules`
inventory coverage. The current local-only continuation adds MLRC-114 through
MLRC-230: owner-switch accessible names, hyphen-safe inline menu editing,
modal-safe/role-correct MenuList help-widget behavior, and owner/public
compliance-date parity for cache-serialized Firebase Admin timestamps, with
focused regression coverage, plus the exact inventory-evidence pointer refresh
required after those source-line changes, a fail-closed MenuList-only local
owner browser fixture, isolated configurable emulator ports, truthful
customer-link recovery, and a corrected first-publication state boundary.
The continuation now also includes real owner/staff lifecycle coverage,
permission-consistent desktop/mobile staff controls, Billing and Transactions
accessibility corrections, Business Health console/keyboard corrections,
scoped Official Business Link analytics, multi-location bootstrap recovery,
and a hosted disposable public-feedback submit, owner-inbox retry,
resolve/undo/re-resolve pass.
The current local-only continuation now extends through MLRC-211, including a
real-browser keyboard pass over the corrected menu reorder controls and cleanup
of stale batch-job listener errors during Projects logout, plus a separately
discovered desktop generation-default accessibility correction and a named
mobile Find & Fix close action. MLRC-166 also replaces Menu Design's
library-synthesized style cards with native selected-state controls and verifies
the reversible flow at 390×844. MLRC-167 closes the remaining mobile icon-only
and symbol-only accessible-name gap across 40 controls, including six controls
whose former `ariaLabel` prop did not emit an `aria-label` in the browser.
MLRC-168 removes a contradictory Customer App readiness claim: enabling the
setting no longer reports customer installation or prompting as active until a
tenant subdomain or custom-domain install origin actually exists.
MLRC-205 extends the shared mobile-navigation clearance to ten adjacent owner
action docks after real pointer testing showed Brand Settings Save could
activate the underlying Share navigation. Brand Settings and Working Hours
reversible drafts, plus Time Slots validation/create/delete with deterministic
cleanup, now pass at 390×844 without cross-navigation.
MLRC-206 prevents MenuList-only Help Centre and Documentation views from
attempting an optional Answerlattice ticket read or subscription without an
admitted Answerlattice session scope. MLRC-207 reconciles the visible mobile
Print Menu action with the More-screen permission resolver; the owner now
reaches the truthful export/no-menu state instead of entering a denial-toast
CSSMotion update loop and the application error boundary.
MLRC-208 removes a duplicate mobile Billing History action and replaces the
click-only billing-help card with one native, named keyboard-operable button.
The provider-free reseller entitlement still renders read-only; the one history
action and the help route both pass in connected local Chrome.
MLRC-209 gives the mobile Transactions date inputs stable names and exposes
the selected action filter through `aria-pressed`; the empty-state, filter,
validation, reset, refresh, and help paths pass without a provider or write.
MLRC-210 removes a shared public-attribution hydration mismatch when equivalent
local hosts differ between server markup and the browser. Menu Board,
Highlights, and the actionable Official Business Page attribution now pass in
connected local Chrome with the allowlisted acquisition tuple and no Next.js
issue overlay.
MLRC-211 closes the adjacent Digital Screens propagation race: every owner
screen-setting mutation now expires the exact private screen-token cache tag
before returning success. Rapid reversible custom-slide-priority changes and
immediate Highlights reloads now acknowledge the current content version
without the former stale-version `409`; both owner mode cards converge to
`Latest update seen` after a fresh read.
MLRC-265 closes a Customer App public-truth divergence found during the
desktop persistence pass: clearing the localized home-screen name now deletes
the stored override, so the public manifest returns to its generated fallback
instead of leaving the owner UI falsely pristine while the old name remains.
MLRC-266 gives the desktop Customer App language, home-screen-name, and
install-link controls stable programmatic names and verifies the reversible
English-reference-to-Hindi draft flow with exact fixture cleanup.
MLRC-267 restores explicit success wording after a desktop subdomain
availability check. The owner now sees that the candidate is available instead
of only seeing the candidate URL beside a newly appeared Save action.
MLRC-268 makes the destructive outlet confirmation and both outlet-rename
fields addressable by purpose. The existing confirmation, redirect warning,
draft, cancellation, tenant/store, and mutation contracts remain unchanged.
MLRC-269 removes the Answerlattice support application from MenuList owner
routes. MenuList now owns a bounded Home, FAQ, and email-contact surface;
legacy `/help-center/kb`, `/ticket`, and `/feedback` deep links recover to FAQ
or Contact without invoking an Answerlattice DAL, API, listener, provider, or
Firebase client. The full governed Answerlattice Help Center remains available
only on its Answerlattice route.
MLRC-270 reduces the generated Complete Menu Kit from 46,453,264 bytes to
1,640,055 bytes by enabling lossless PDF stream and embedded-PNG compression
in all five governed printable-asset render paths. The fixed ZIP retains the
same eleven files, passes archive integrity checks, and preserves the exact
A5-fold, A4, and A6 PDF page dimensions.
MLRC-271 removes an owner-distribution blocker from Counter Sticker output.
The generated `SCAN MENU` layer formerly crossed the live QR code; the corrected
layout gives the business identity, call to action, QR panel, and attribution
separate vertical regions while retaining the governed 945×945, 8×8 cm output.
MLRC-272 makes Saved design creation idempotent across an interrupted local
Storage request. While one save is pending, the editor cannot be closed, a
repeated Save receives an explicit wait acknowledgement, and every overlapping
attempt shares one reserved template identity. The saved title now comes from
the edited document rather than the original platform-template title.
MLRC-273 gives the Saved design destructive confirmation its visible title as
its programmatic dialog name. The bridge is mounted inside the static Ant
confirmation title because `App.useApp().modal.confirm` does not preserve the
outer `modalRender` hook used by the older helper.
MLRC-274 names the Creative Editor inspector's lock, duplicate, delete, seven
background-alignment, and gradient-stop removal controls. The enabled actions
were then exercised in the real embedded Counter Sticker editor and the
browser-local layer/geometry changes were discarded through Close editor.
MLRC-275 removes two false Background-panel affordances: a permanently checked
read-only Show background checkbox and a no-op Color button. Color background
is now truthful status, while the real image-layer navigation remains an
explicit Add image layer action beside the existing Solid/Gradient controls.
MLRC-212 through MLRC-216 complete the adjacent Digital Screens upload and
multi-location pass. Mobile now preserves the governed prepared-media identity,
the adjustment dialog is named, every owner request follows an independently
authorized active store, the strict add-slide transport omits the client
`Timestamp`, Highlights serializes expiry before the React client boundary,
ambiguous failed-add responses are reconciled before any media cleanup, and a
successful slide removal cleans its referenced immutable Storage object. A
real mobile upload, caption update, owner-only toggle, branch public render,
main-store isolation check, confirmation/cancel path, exact cache propagation,
and deterministic fixture cleanup pass locally.
MLRC-217 repairs the same selected-outlet boundary in External Menu Sync. A
mapped branch no longer fails its protected secret load against the login
store; the route derives tenant authority from the session, admits only the
login or explicitly mapped store, and rechecks the canonical selected-store
permission before secret work. The provider-free disabled state, validation,
reset, and typed-rotation confirmation/cancel paths pass locally without a
secret mutation or provider request.
MLRC-218 names the full-height mobile Privacy, Terms, and Refund policy dialogs
from their visible policy titles. Connected Chrome uniquely resolved all three
dialogs, exercised baseline disclosure and edit/cancel recovery, rejected a
nine-character custom policy before any write, and discarded the surrounding
Official Page draft without saving.
MLRC-219 completes the provider-free reseller pre-checkout journey on mobile
and desktop with one disposable emulator-only reseller login. It exposes
selected mobile plan state and the location-count name, removes the desktop
disconnected-form console warning, includes commitment in desktop confirmation,
and redirects a reseller's direct `/dashboard` request to `/reseller` instead
of an empty owner workspace. Both submit controls remained uninvoked; exact
Auth/user/profile cleanup and credential-file removal were verified.
MLRC-220 closes the reported local authentication and unpaid-owner recovery
evidence gap. A disposable emulator owner signed in with an upper-cased email
and leading/trailing whitespace around both credentials, reached the mobile
owner workspace, then exercised the no-subscription gate, View Plans, mobile
Billing, pricing, and the complete Pro billing-details draft up to—but not
through—the excluded Razorpay handoff. Restoring the deterministic amount-zero
reseller-style Pro entitlement removed the gate on a fresh dashboard load.
MLRC-221 closes a keyboard recovery defect in the shared mobile selector
boundary. Escape from an open item-detail selector formerly closed the complete
item editor and discarded its draft. The selector now consumes the first
Escape and closes only its own list; the item draft remains intact, while a
second Escape retains the expected editor-close behavior. The adjacent
nutrition, dietary, variant, visibility, merchandising, Reset, Back, and fresh
public-truth paths pass at 390×844 without a write.
MLRC-222 closes a mobile category icon-picker performance and accessibility
defect. Opening the picker formerly mounted 1,251 buttons at once. The current
picker renders 120 of 1,215 icons initially, exposes a 44px progressive-load
action, resets the bound for search, suppresses unrelated suggestions during
search, names the search field explicitly, and exposes selected Icons/Emoji
state. The adjacent category add, hide, rename, Reset, Save, delete-cancel,
delete-confirm, and fresh public-menu convergence journey passed against the
isolated emulator fixture; the disposable category was removed.
MLRC-223 names every regular-hours and special-hours input from its visible
purpose. Connected Chrome at 390×844 uniquely resolved and focused the all-day,
per-day, date, occasion, and special opening/closing controls. A reversible
Friday 09:00–22:00 save was cancelled once, then acknowledged, retained after
fresh owner navigation, and immediately rendered `Closed · Opens at 09:00 AM`
on a fresh public menu. A second acknowledged save restored Friday to Closed;
fresh public menu and Official Business Page reads returned to no hours claim.
MLRC-224 closes a certification-tool integrity gap: both evidence-bearing
working-hours sources now participate in the local owner source manifest, so
any later source drift invalidates their exact control evidence instead of
silently retaining a stale pass.
MLRC-225 names the Today quick-hours dialog, its opening/closing fields, and
the shared custom-message and exact-expiry fields. Connected Chrome uniquely
resolved every affected control; the custom temporary-status draft and dialog
Cancel paths wrote nothing. A reversible Friday `00:01–22:00` quick-hours save
changed Today to Open and a cache-busted public HQ read to `Open` without
affecting the branch, then the full editor restored Friday to Closed and fresh
owner/public reads returned to the original no-hours state.
MLRC-226 aligns the full editor's dirty-state comparison with its serialized
working-hours contract. Closing a day that retained hidden editor times no
longer leaves Reset and Save Hours falsely enabled after acknowledged
persistence. A post-fix Open→Closed toggle disabled both actions immediately
without a write.
MLRC-227 names all three Menu Manager composer Popups from their visible
titles: Start from, Work on, and Suggestions. The provider-free journey entered
Menu Manager from More, exercised both sheet branches and their named recovery
actions, drafted the nested `Change working hours for today` suggestion, and
cleared it. Send was never invoked, so no AI/provider, proposal, credit, or
project mutation ran.
MLRC-228 closes a certification-inventory integrity gap. The old line-oriented
menu-action regex omitted multiline keyed actions such as More > Menu setup and
also misclassified unrelated `items = [` declarations as controls. The
generator and verifier now derive keyed `onClick`/`onPress`/`onAction` object
actions from the TypeScript AST. The already exercised Menu setup shortcut now
has its exact passing row, while false declaration rows are removed.
MLRC-229 names the Social Media platform picker and nested link editor from
their visible titles. Empty Save now retains the editor and explains that a
public link is required instead of silently closing an unchanged blank draft.
The adjacent More shortcuts for Temporary Status, Special Menus, Menu Design,
Business details, and Social Media all reach their intended mobile surfaces;
no social link or special menu was saved.
MLRC-230 removes the Presence Monitor's five invalid nested row/button
interactions and its decorative unnamed button. Each passive discovery row now
has one named 44px setup action, and the setup Popup is named from the selected
platform. The provider-free Search & Discovery route pass reached Domain,
Business Copy Setup, SEO and AEO, and Presence Monitor without a write or
external-platform action.
MLRC-231 repairs the desktop Search & Discovery navigation label. The active
Ant Menu item placed a block-level `Flex` inside its inline title container, so
the visible label and `5 gaps` status were laid out below the 40px menu-item
box even though the accessibility tree still named the control. The label now
uses an inline-flex container and remains fully visible inside the active row.
Current-run before/after screenshots are stored under
`__docs__/audits/evidence/menulist-rc-desktop-business-settings-2026-08-28/`.
MLRC-232 keeps expected browser AI-request failures inside the existing
owner-facing recovery instead of also raising the Next.js development issue
overlay. Browser diagnostics remain bounded and support-readable at log level;
server failures retain error-level secure logging. The failed provider-free
cover attempt consumed zero credits and changed no media, store, or public
truth.
MLRC-233 removes the duplicate ambiguous Ant stepper buttons from Established
Year, Google Rating, and Review Count while preserving their uniquely named
spinbutton semantics and keyboard increment behavior. A connected-Chrome
ArrowUp/Reset pass restored the unchanged draft with no write; screenshot
`05-official-page-number-inputs-fixed.png` records the corrected surface.
MLRC-234 repairs the desktop Business Attributes Reset contract. Ant's nested
form update merged an empty persisted object into the changed draft, so missing
attribute keys were never cleared. Reset now clears every known nested key to
its exact persisted boolean-or-undefined value without changing the normal
save projection. The complete 17-switch draft/reset pass writes nothing.
MLRC-235 removes 462 non-interactive Ant `Form.Item`, `Form.List`, and
`Form.ErrorList` containers from the control inventory. Those components group
labels, validation, and nested values; they cannot be triggered by a user. The
verifier now rejects their return, three false auth evidence claims were
removed, and 11 genuinely hidden MenuList form fields are explicitly retained
as non-user-triggerable rather than silently treated as tested controls.
MLRC-236 closes the console-error edge exposed by MLRC-234's first correction.
The complete reset used 17 sequential nested `setFieldValue` calls; Ant restored
the visual truth but emitted `Warning: There may be circular references` from
its equality guard. Reset now replaces the complete governed nested attribute
projection in one field update. Connected Chrome restored the Vegetarian draft
to unchecked with no issue overlay or console warning, and no Save or Firebase
write occurred.
MLRC-237 extends that recovery to persisted-absent optional scalar fields. A
reversible GSTIN draft survived Reset because `setFieldsValue` received no
`gstn` key when the stored document omitted it. The centralized projection now
includes every optional Business Profile and Official Page field as an explicit
value-or-undefined key. Connected Chrome restored Brand name, Location name,
Business Type, Business Email, and GSTIN together after invalid-email recovery;
no issue overlay, console warning, Save acknowledgement, or Firebase write
occurred.
MLRC-238 closes the same persisted-absent recovery gap for nested analytics
identifiers. Invalid GA4, Search Console, and Meta Pixel drafts correctly
blocked Save, but the first Reset retained all three because their persisted
analytics object omitted those keys. The normalized analytics projection now
includes each optional identifier explicitly. Connected Chrome then cleared
all three drafts and errors and restored the five tracking-category switches
to their exact persisted truth without a Save, Firebase write, or issue overlay.
MLRC-239 closes the equivalent computed-default recovery gap for locale fields.
The first five-control pass showed timezone and currency restoring while
business-day end, date format, and time format retained cancelled drafts because
their persisted keys were absent and an initialization effect did not rerun.
Initial load and Reset now share one locale-default projection. Connected Chrome
restored Asia/Kolkata, the restaurant 3:00 AM boundary, the configured date and
12-hour time formats, INR/₹, and the default India country without a write or
issue overlay.
The adjacent special-hours lifecycle now also passes at 390×844. Empty-date
submission produced the bounded recovery with no write; a disposable future
closed date was added, edited to today's `00:01–22:00` window, and immediately

Remove deleted it. Fresh owner navigation returned `No special dates added`

The adjacent provider-free category-scheduling lifecycle now also passes at
390×844. A disposable 09:00–17:00 preset was created and assigned to Drinks;

The adjacent desktop Edit Category pass additionally exercised reversible
name, active-state, and time-visibility drafts plus inline preset validation.
Creating `QA Lunch` intentionally wrote the store-level preset immediately;

MLRC-169 names all 28 shared mobile owner selectors discovered across locale,
operations, reseller, scheduler, special-menu, app-settings, bulk-edit,

MLRC-170 names the Time Slots add/edit dialog and its Name, Start Time, and End

MLRC-171 names the App Settings dialog and all 13 theme-colour choices, exposes

MLRC-172 removes the App Settings library colour popup whose internal sliders
and fields had no accessible names, replacing it with one named 44px native

MLRC-173 names all eight Mobile SEO/Analytics text inputs and both Analytics
dialogs. MLRC-174 applies the same shared GA4, Search Console, and Meta Pixel
normalization contract to desktop owner validation, mobile owner validation,
and public script rendering, so an owner can no longer receive a false success

fail safely when a store has a project but no public subdomain/custom domain:

Domain settings instead of throwing from `generateProjectUrl`.
MLRC-177 repairs the shared mobile `TextArea` boundary after real Chrome proved
that caller-supplied `aria-label` values were discarded. The wrapper now
forwards the name to the native textarea, and all 24 mobile-owner textarea call
sites carry explicit action-specific names.
MLRC-178 prevents malformed subdomain and custom-domain availability inputs
from enabling either desktop or mobile backend checks, preserving server-side
authority while eliminating avoidable requests for syntactically invalid data.
It also adds a manifest-bound local non-dynamic page-render verifier so current
HTTP evidence cannot be applied to stale or unknown routes, plus exact
runtime-reader classification for dormant feature declarations and complete
environment-conditional Firebase Function export discovery. It now also
contains source-manifest-bound local desktop/mobile control evidence and the
first-publish save-state correction: an unpublished menu reports `Draft` and
explains that it remains private, while later published saves report `Live`.
These changes have not been committed,
pushed, or deployed.
The current local-only continuation now extends through MLRC-189. MLRC-179
prevents owner-facing Official Business Page and Google-listing links from

refreshes both the durable NextAuth session and Firebase Auth claims after a

switch action beside the independent Rename and Deactivate controls. MLRC-185
names all three Mobile Locations sheets and the three form inputs
whose placeholders had been their only browser-visible labels. MLRC-186 gives
the shared mobile static confirmation boundary a stable browser-accessible name
derived from its visible title and verifies the real disposable rename and
deactivate mutations plus deterministic fixture restoration. MLRC-187 updates
the Special Menu lifecycle source verifier to assert the Dashboard's stronger
scope-key, stale-state rejection, and tenant/store-scoped persistence contract
instead of the retired unscoped dependency-array text. MLRC-188 gives every
Customer Message Copy, native Share, and WhatsApp action a localized accessible
name at the shared mobile card boundary. MLRC-189 forwards each visible mobile
QR-sheet title to the shared dialog boundary, covering Direct Menu, Official
Business, install, store, feedback, design, project, and outlet QR surfaces.
MLRC-190 removes nested category/item row controls from the Mobile Menu
disclosure and list semantics. MLRC-191 names the shared item-editor dialog and
its previously placeholder-only price/nutrition/variant fields. MLRC-192 keeps
an explicit non-image item Save busy until the queued Firestore write and
public-cache invalidation have both completed, instead of reporting success and
closing while persistence is still pending. MLRC-193 extends that same
acknowledged boundary to item create/delete and category

their semantics promise. MLRC-195 names the bulk-action dialogs and selectors,
removes nested and pointer-only selection controls, and requires every bulk
mutation path to await the shared acknowledged project-write boundary before
closing. A reversible real-browser pass covered availability, visibility,
fixed pricing, and category move against disposable emulator data; each change
propagated to a fresh public menu, and the original one-category/one-item
fixture was restored.
MLRC-196 names the complete mobile project-selector dialog/form surface,
separates Select, Manage, and Create into native keyboard controls, and proves
the provider-free create, duplicate, activation, switching, edit/restore, and
deletion lifecycle against disposable emulator projects. Both temporary menus

MLRC-197 repairs the provider-free Special Menu lifecycle discovered through
the real 390x844 owner flow: create/edit fields and dialogs are named, mounted
guards remain live under React Strict Mode, a late-arriving base-menu selection
hydrates the form without resetting its schedule, created menus are cached
before selection, edit results converge from the authoritative response, and
end/cancel transitions immediately restore the base menu. Active and scheduled
creation, edit/reopen, end, cancel, owner selection, and public propagation all
passed against disposable emulator projects. MLRC-198 removes mixed-shape
summary remnants during project deletion and ignores incomplete historical
summary rows, preventing deleted special menus from reappearing as selectable
`Untitled` menus. The final selector and public menu were restored exactly.
MLRC-199 names all three Mobile Staff dialogs, exposes role choice state, and
re-arms the screen's mounted settlement guard during React Strict Mode replay.
A disposable name-only staff account completed provider-free creation, role
change/restore, session revocation, one-time passcode reset, deactivate, and

guard that rendered an Activate action but silently rejected already-inactive

MLRC-201 applies the same Strict Mode settlement correction to Mobile Roles,
names the role editor and its controls, removes nested permission interactions,
exposes read-only permission state, and completes a disposable custom-role
create/edit/deactivate/reactivate lifecycle. The real removal pass also proved
that the former mobile **Delete This Role** action only soft-deactivated the
role, claimed deletion, and immediately retained the row as Off. That

switch remains the reversible role lifecycle control. The disposable role was

MLRC-202 keeps the mobile Official Business Page action bar above the fixed
bottom navigation, preventing a Save tap from activating Share. MLRC-203 makes

Concurrent Answerlattice certification and append-only Git-ledger updates are
present in the shared worktree and are preserved without attribution to this

**Current hosted QA base state:** a cache-busted `/api/version` readback on
August 26 proved exact staging base commit
`59e1dc2561750ed2d97764fa30df7614c3071fd2`, verified provenance, and deployment

triggered by that readback. A separately authorized consolidated release moved
local and server `staging` to `4586bb8523a92b1c7d0ea37a5b17267432346cf6`.
Its one automatic Vercel QA deployment was canceled by the configured Ignored
Build Step before build execution, consuming no build CPU, and a fresh
cache-busted readback still returned served build `59e1dc256…`. Therefore
MLRC-104 through MLRC-113 are committed on `staging` but are not represented as
hosted-verified on the newer commit. This
certification session triggered no push or deployment. The currently served
MenuList runtime includes

Kits inventory, missing-hours truth, terminal used-state, and legacy-null
projection corrections while `main` remains untouched.

and the Today guide/custom-status recovery corrections from

`2362f794eb61c908228c0f4956cd03b19d5eb3bd`, the empty-project editor load
boundary from `314ff97cb632f60505a8a10658c3d8c89ff5350f`, and the Growth Kits
accessible selector names from `c81dc4bba8460f02209aecae1150b7463325bb7f`
are included. `main`
remains untouched.

**Certification date:** August 28, 2026

**Canonical staging ledger:** [MenuList staging feature certification](../deployment/menulist-staging-feature-certification.md)
**External gate runbook:** [External certification runbook](../production-readiness/external-certification-runbook.md)

This document is the release-candidate summary and decision record. It does not
replace the existing feature-level staging ledger or external-evidence runbook.
The generated discovery inventory is retained in
[`menulist-rc-certification-inventory.csv`](./menulist-rc-certification-inventory.csv).

## A. Certification scope

- Product: MenuList only.
- Environments: local source, Firebase demo emulators, controlled local browser,
  and the MenuList QA application/tenant hosts after exact build identity is
  verified.
- Production customer data and destructive production testing are excluded.
- Canonica, Answerlattice, CampaignCue, SignalDesk, MyCodex, standalone
  GrowthOS product behavior, KitStamp, and Neelvara behavior is excluded except
  for routing and product-separation checks. MenuList's shipped owner-facing
  Growth Kits add-on remains fully included even though its internal namespace
  uses `growthos`.
- Live Razorpay checkout, credentials, webhooks, captures, refunds,
  settlements, and money movement are excluded. Application behavior around
  the provider boundary is included through source, deterministic tests,
  emulator fixtures, and mock lifecycle states.
- Initial fixture classes: unauthenticated visitor, new/no-tenant user,
  existing owner, staff/restricted actor, public customer, valid/invalid
  tenant and store, enabled/disabled feature, and material subscription and
  lifecycle states derived from current runtime guards. A fresh disposable
  hosted QA owner is active at tenant `9`, HQ store `13`, and branch store `14`,
  with a labelled provider-free zero-value non-payment entitlement, three-store
  prepaid capacity, 225 credits, and subdomain `qa-rc-32df613a32`. Its bounded
  lease expires on August 30, 2026. Firebase
  Admin readback, Firebase Web Auth, exact owner claims, tenant/store shape,
  the production active-subscription query, and the shared entitlement
  predicate all passed. Its hosted credential is not reproduced here. The
  prior fixtures remain separately marker-bounded and were
  not modified or represented as the new browser credential. The isolated local
  reseller credential existed only in a mode-600 temporary file during the
  browser pass and was deleted afterward; no password appears in the repository,
  report, terminal output, or shell history.
- After the operator completed local Firebase CLI reauthentication, a fresh
  provider-free hosted-QA certification fixture was created under marker
  `ml-hosted-qa-certification-3e1099df5f`: tenant `10`, store `15`, and
  subdomain `qa-rc-3e1099df5f`, expiring September 1, 2026. Firebase Admin and
  Web client verification passed the exact owner claims, tenant/store scope,
  one active subscription, and reseller-backed entitlement. Its generated
  credential remains outside the repository in a mode-600 temporary file and
  is not reproduced in this report. No payment, Razorpay action, infrastructure
  deployment, staging push, Vercel build, or production mutation occurred.

## B. Inventory summary

The current generator discovered 6,610 source candidates across the shared
repository. These are discovery candidates, not passing test claims.

| Pages | 312 | Product classification and runtime reachability in progress |
| Route handlers | 295 | Product classification, methods, and boundary tests in progress |
| Layouts | 20 | Source and host behavior in progress |
| Loading/error/not-found surfaces | 9 | Runtime coverage in progress |
| User-control candidates | 5,612 | 3,774 MenuList controls are classified, of which 3,634 map to page routes; 140 MenuList controls are explicitly classified as non-shipped source; 41 hidden/permanently disabled controls are source-verified as non-user-triggerable; 135 exact physical controls carry hosted interaction evidence; 1,837 Creative Editor/website/auth/pricing/contact/create-menu/tool/recovery/owner/reseller/platform/public-customer controls carry current source-manifest-bound local desktop/mobile interaction evidence. Existing evidence remains attached to concrete controls under the correct product boundary. Current local evidence additionally covers the invalid Menu Preview recovery link, shared rich-text heading, alignment, formatting, list, quote, divider, normalized/rejected link, rejected image, and complete draft-table controls with all test drafts discarded, plus the Platform Notifications to populated Ops Control Room recovery path and five populated Ops monitor round trips. Exactly 1,621 MenuList controls remain honestly `NOT_RUN`. |
| Feature flags | 334 | All declarations, boolean helper results, and 1,201 source reader locations pass current source-manifest-bound registry checks. Of 195 MenuList-classified declarations, 27 have no runtime reader and are closed as dormant/non-shipped; the other 168 carry honest partial registry/reader evidence while their individual flag-on/flag-off journeys remain pending. |
| Firebase Function exports | 28 | 23 MenuList-owned exports plus five fail-closed Answerlattice compatibility boundaries; deployed-only and emulator-only conditional exports are both inventoried. All 23 MenuList wrappers now have source-hash-bound local runtime evidence: three intentionally retired callables pass their complete current local contract and twenty remain partial pending applicable success/deployed evidence. |

Current product classification yielded 4,283 MenuList candidates and 2,327
separation-boundary candidates. A candidate remains `DISCOVERED_UNTESTED` until
its rendered reachability and behavior are proven; source inspection alone does
not mark a control tested.

The August 29 matrix readback is explicit: 1,621/4,283 MenuList candidates
remain `NOT_RUN`, all of them user-control candidates. Of the 3,774 MenuList
controls, 2,153 have either exact interaction evidence or a source-unreachable,
non-shipped/non-user-triggerable classification. Existing source, contract, emulator, route-render,
feature-registry, and representative hosted evidence is valuable adjacent
coverage, but it does not convert the remaining control rows into interaction
passes. This count is a completion-gate blocker and prevents any claim that
every discovered control has been certified.

All 168 MenuList feature flags with runtime readers now carry
`PASS_PARTIAL_FLAG_REGISTRY_BOUNDARY`: their declarations resolve to booleans,
both registry helpers return the declared value, readers reference only declared
flags, runtime source does not mutate the registry, and rate-limiting environment
defaults/overrides are deterministic. This status deliberately does not claim
that every individual feature's enabled and disabled user journey has run.

The 23 MenuList Function rows are no longer `NOT_RUN`: three intentionally
retired callables carry `PASS_LOCAL_DEPLOYED_RETEST_PENDING`, while the other
twenty carry `PARTIAL_LOCAL_RUNTIME_BOUNDARY`. Those statuses prove exact
compiled-wrapper execution without claiming provider-success, active-store,
deployed-trigger success, or current deployed-source parity.

The 49 MenuList private pages are now classified from their inherited route
guards and shell contracts: 24 owner/authorized-staff pages, 23 platform-admin
pages, and 2 platform-or-reseller pages. Billing and both Help Center route
shapes are the only recovery routes available across active, Starter, unpaid,
pending, and expired states. `/platform/test-sentry` is the sole intentionally
desktop-only private page; every other private page is mapped to the shared
desktop and MobileShell contract. These classifications are inventory truth,
not proof that every rendered control has been exercised.

All 49 MenuList private page routes plus ten legacy Answerlattice control-plane
separation routes have a current connected-Chrome signed-out
access result. Every route reached `/signin` with its exact intended callback;
`/qrCode`, `/ops`, `/ops/extraction`, and `/ops/scheduler` preserved their
canonical destination. The catch-all Help Center page was exercised through
`/help-center/kb`. These rows are marked
`ACCESS_PASSED_FUNCTIONAL_INTERACTION_PENDING`, so authentication protection is
proved without claiming their authenticated controls were exercised.

Of 3,774 MenuList-classified control candidates, 3,634 map through the static
import graph to one or more App Router pages. The remaining 140 controls in 46
source files have no page import path and now carry the explicit final status
`SOURCE_UNREACHABLE_NOT_USER_TRIGGERABLE`. They are classified as non-shipped
source, not represented as runtime-tested controls. Shared components retain
all reachable page routes so cross-product or cross-role reuse is visible in
the matrix.

All 140 MenuList route handlers now have resolved exported HTTP methods and an
explicit source-derived access-boundary classification. This includes the four
Growth Kits APIs because active product truth defines Growth Kits as a shipped
MenuList Pro/Multi-location add-on, not a standalone product. An exact-hosted
anonymous runtime pass exercised all 157 exported methods with credentials
omitted and empty or invalid deterministic input: 5 returned public/static 200
responses, 1 redirected, 15 rejected with 400, 130 with 401, 1 with 403, and 5
with 404.
There were no final timeouts or 5xx responses, and no protected method returned
2xx. The five legacy
`/api/widget/*` handlers are classified as Answerlattice separation boundaries,
not MenuList APIs. Valid authenticated and successful public behavior remains
separately tracked and is not implied by this anonymous boundary pass.

No certification defect is closed merely from pre-existing source history.

audit-fix-retest loop.

## E. Firebase cost audit

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-333 storeless platform bootstrap

- **Original behavior:** every hard load of a valid storeless platform session
  entered the owner tenant/store bootstrap and attempted at least one unusable
  tenant read before failing.
- **Change:** tenant/store/subscription bootstrap now requires an admitted
  `storeId`. Storeless platform sessions perform zero owner bootstrap reads;
  owner sessions retain the existing scoped path.
- **Correctness/security:** platform authorization is unchanged, and the
  auth-security matrix retains the owner/store boundary.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-336 and MLRC-338 invalid canonical entities

- **Original behavior:** a blank tenant could enter ID reservation and tenant
  persistence; a blank asset entity could enter preview preparation/upload and
  Firestore persistence.
- **Change:** trimmed-name admission now fails before tenant ID reservation and
  before any asset preview upload. The browser also blocks these requests.
- **Before/after:** the exercised invalid drafts now perform zero Auth,
  Firestore, Storage, Function, or provider mutations. Exact operation counts
  for a formerly completed invalid write are not claimed because the fixed
  browser pass intentionally did not invoke it.
- **Correctness/security:** validation is duplicated at UI and DAL trust
  boundaries; no rule, authorization, cache, or public-freshness behavior was
  weakened.

### Investigated, no billed-operation reduction claimed — MLRC-341 Scheduler settlement reads

- The former bounded list was denied by the existing private-document rule and
  therefore produced no usable settlement state. The replacement reuses the
  already-loaded admitted store list and performs at most one exact document
  read per store, under the same maximum.
- A successful bounded list and the exact-read implementation both bill by
  documents returned/read, so this is a security-compatible correctness fix,
  not a claimed Firestore billing reduction. It removes failed network/log
  work without adding a second `storesSummary` read or weakening the private
  `platformSummary` boundary.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-349 unchanged tenant save

- **Original behavior:** one unchanged platform tenant Update produced one
  Firestore document write because a cloned, non-editable `storesList` made the
  reference-based draft comparison appear non-empty before that field was
  removed.
- **Change:** remove `storesList` before deciding whether any editable tenant
  field changed. The existing update path is skipped when the resulting patch
  is empty.
- **Before/after:** unchanged Update changed from 1 tenant write to 0. Emulator
  `updateTime` and `modifiedOn` both advanced in the pre-fix reproduction and
  remained exactly unchanged in the corrected retest.
- **Correctness:** no authorization, validation, changed-field persistence,
  logo upload, name propagation, cache, or public-truth behavior was weakened.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-353 unchanged platform-user save

- **Original behavior:** an unchanged user draft entered `updatePlatformUser`
  because cloned store and product-account arrays/objects differed by reference,
  causing one avoidable Firestore update request.
- **Change:** the platform user surface now uses the existing nested-value diff
  boundary and adds the document ID only after a real field difference exists.
- **Before/after:** unchanged Update changed from 1 Firestore update request to
  0. The pre-fix drawer closed through the success path; the corrected drawer
  remains open on the no-change path, and exact emulator `updateTime` remains
  `2026-08-28T19:43:33.340Z`.
- **Correctness:** authorization, tenant/store scope admission, verification,
  active-state, role, mapping, and default-store mutation paths are unchanged.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-342–348 platform controls

- The accessibility corrections, reversible drafts, and confirmation gates add
  no Firestore read/write, listener, Storage, Function, provider, analytics, or
  cache operation. MLRC-347 reuses the existing Business Settings form and
  create DAL only after valid owner input; invalid-name and cancellation tests
  stayed at zero writes. MLRC-348 prevents accidental archival but does not
  change the billed operation count of an explicitly confirmed archive.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-350–354 platform administration

- Store/user scope filters reuse the existing bounded reads; naming and native
  Edit actions do not add requests. Pricing-plan filter and draft interactions
  are client-local. The invalid and cancelled plan pass retained an exact zero
  `pricingPlans` document count and performed no provider or payment operation.
- MLRC-353 is recorded separately as a confirmed avoided write. No batching,
  cache, Razorpay, or remote-build cost claim is made for the remaining fixes.

### Prevented empty-operation work — MLRC-355 Font Presets sorting

- **Original behavior:** an empty or one-font list could open the sequencing
  modal and expose Update despite no possible reordering, allowing an avoidable
  sort DAL call and empty/baseline batch path.
- **Change:** Sort and modal Update remain disabled until at least two fonts are
  present. This adds no read and changes no valid multi-font persistence path.
- **Before/after:** the zero-font UI changed from one actionable sort path to
  zero; exact emulator readback remains zero font documents. No billed write
  reduction is claimed for the cancelled browser pass because Update was not
  invoked pre-fix.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-357 Platform Notifications

- The monitor retains its existing manual-refresh contract: one current-user
  authorization read plus one newest-first scan capped at 150 documents; the
  same window supplies rows, filters, and counts with no listener or aggregate
  count query.
- Accessible names, native row actions, and expected form-error handling add no
  backend operation. The invalid and cancelled browser pass retained an exact
  zero `systemAlerts` document count. No batching, cache, provider, email, or
  WhatsApp cost reduction is claimed.

### Confirmed avoided reseller mutation — MLRC-358

- **Original behavior:** clicking Update on an unchanged reseller profile still
  issued `/api/reseller/manage`. The admitted server path performs profile
  authority/uniqueness checks, Firebase Auth reconciliation, and coordinated
  `resellerProfiles`/`users` persistence even when the intended values have not
  changed.
- **Change:** desktop and mobile compare the normalized editable draft with the
  loaded profile before sending a request. Blank edit passwords are omitted so
  real non-password changes remain schema-valid.
- **Before/after:** unchanged Update changed from 1 management POST to 0 network,
  Auth, Firestore-read, and Firestore-write operations. Exact emulator profile
  and user `updateTime` values remained unchanged through the corrected no-op.
- **Correctness:** actual active-state changes still executed the complete
  server boundary; browser and Admin-emulator readback proved Auth disable and
  re-enable plus `RESELLER` custom-claim convergence.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-359–361 reseller access truth

- Hard post-login navigation prevents a stale pre-login session shell but adds
  no Firebase read/write/listener/Storage/Function operation beyond the existing
  authenticated app load.
- Hiding offline-only controls while the canonical flag is false prevents
  guaranteed-rejected API attempts; no Firestore saving is claimed because the
  server already rejects those operations before their business-data mutation.
- Returning a denied reseller-management deep link to `/reseller` changes only
  route recovery. Platform authorization, reseller read caps, and the three
  bounded dashboard reads remain unchanged.

### Confirmed avoided store write — MLRC-362

- **Original behavior:** every unchanged desktop/platform Business Settings
  submit generated a new business-copy manual-override timestamp before the
  changed-leaf comparison. The resulting non-empty patch invoked `updateStore`,
  updated `modifiedOn`, and requested public-cache revalidation even though no
  owner-visible or public-visible business truth changed.
- **Measured before:** each repeated no-change click advanced the exact emulator
  `stores/99611.modifiedOn`. A guarded before/after trace identified only
  `businessCopyMeta.lastManualOverrideAt` and `modifiedOn` as changed keys.
- **Change:** changed-leaf projection now precedes business-copy audit metadata;
  metadata is appended only when the projected update contains a real governed
  copy-field change.
- **Measured after:** repeated unchanged Save Changes retained the exact prior
  `modifiedOn` and `lastManualOverrideAt`, reducing the flow from one store write
  plus one revalidation request per click to zero. A real tagline change and a
  non-copy city change remain independently covered by the regression suite.
- **Correctness:** no cache was disabled. Actual store changes continue through
  the existing authorization, update, summary, and precise invalidation path.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-363

- Platform store-drawer mount previously issued one owner-secret HTTP request
  that was denied by selected-store admission before the POS rate limiter,
  Firestore transaction, secret read, or write. Removing the inapplicable
  platform surface avoids the rejected request and runtime issue, but no billed
  Firebase-operation reduction is claimed.
- The owner route still performs its documented bounded server-secret reads
  when an authorized owner opens External Menu Sync. No secret access, tenant
  rule, cache, or provider behavior was weakened.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-364

- The change is a scope-correctness and fail-closed authorization repair. It
  adds no Firestore read, write, listener, Function, Storage, provider, cache,
  or analytics operation to valid store reads.
- A conflicting store document is now rejected at the first shared reader
  instead of mounting a partially functional owner screen whose protected APIs
  must reject it later. This can avoid downstream rejected requests, but no
  billed-operation reduction is claimed because the protected request was
  already denied before business-data work.
- The two-document repair was limited to the disposable local emulator fixture;
  it is certification setup evidence, not a deploy, migration, production
  mutation, or claimed product-cost optimization.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-365

- The correction changes browser accessibility metadata only. It adds no
  Firestore read/write/listener, Function, Storage, analytics, provider, cache,
  or revalidation operation.
- The feature-on pass reused the existing bounded project summary and Past
  Activity reads; the feature flag was restored to false afterward, and the
  disabled direct route again redirected before mounting the history readers.
  No Firebase cost reduction is claimed.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-366

- This is a certification-inventory and product-separation correction only.
  It changes no page runtime, Firestore query, write, listener, Function,
  Storage operation, provider request, cache, or revalidation path.
- Moving eleven legacy Answerlattice pages out of the MenuList denominator
  prevents false QA effort and false readiness claims, but it does not reduce a
  billed Firebase operation. The current anonymous 124-route MenuList smoke
  performed HTTP reads only and admitted no authenticated data mutation.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-367

- This correction changes only the local certification generator, verifier,
  evidence identities, matrix, and report. It does not alter a rendered
  component, API, DAL, Firestore read/write/listener, Function, Storage path,
  analytics event, cache, revalidation path, provider request, or entitlement.
- Removing duplicate audit rows reduces false certification work but does not
  reduce a billed Firebase operation, so no runtime cost saving is claimed.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-368

- This is a per-control certification classification correction. The shared
  Mobile More runtime, role gates, navigation, and all destination screens are
  unchanged, so it adds or removes no Firestore read/write/listener, Function,
  Storage operation, provider request, cache entry, or revalidation.
- Keeping Answerlattice-only actions out of MenuList prevents cross-product QA
  claims but does not change billed Firebase work; no cost reduction is claimed.

### Investigated, no Firebase optimization claimed — MLRC-369

- This correction changes only certification classification for existing
  historical Answerlattice component families. It does not add, remove, or
  modify their runtime imports, Firestore reads/writes/listeners, Functions,
  Storage operations, caches, analytics, provider calls, or revalidation.
- The smaller MenuList denominator is an evidence-correctness improvement, not
  a billed-operation reduction; no Firebase cost optimization is claimed.

### Confirmed reliability/cost containment — MLRC-370 logout listener teardown

- **Original behavior:** an expected `permission-denied` callback could arrive
  after Firebase Auth cleared but before both Projects image-batch listeners
  unmounted. The callback stopped the loader but also emitted failure handling
  and scheduled owner-visible recovery for a session that was already gone.
- **Change:** the listener boundary now recognizes only that exact no-user auth
  teardown and exits after bounded diagnostic and loader cleanup. Signed-in
  permission failures and every other error retain the existing recovery path.

### Client no-op and Firebase cost-risk correction — MLRC-381

- **Original behavior:** a numerically unchanged fixed-price request remained an
  actionable Command Center mutation. Applying it marked the editor dirty and
  scheduled the existing autosave comparison; a differently formatted but
  numerically equivalent stored price could also be normalized and persisted.
- **Before/after operation estimate:** the deterministic exact-string fixture
  would have reached one unnecessary client autosave comparison and then been
  stopped by the existing semantic project comparator, so its billed Firestore
  count remained zero. Equivalent formatted-price states carried a possible one
  project-write plus cache/public-refresh risk. The corrected no-op is blocked
  before Apply, leaving zero autosave attempt, zero write, and zero invalidation.
- The pure preview now removes unchanged item and attribute prices while
  preserving attribute-only real changes. Connected Chrome proves fixed ₹80 is
  disabled and fixed ₹90 remains actionable. Because the safe browser pass did
  not apply the pre-fix no-op or observe a billed write, this is recorded as a
  correctness and cost-risk correction rather than a measured Firebase billing
  reduction.

### Correctness-first write restoration — MLRC-382

- **Original behavior:** shared editor modal callbacks replaced the persisted
  comparison baseline before autosave. A real reorder could therefore produce
  zero project writes and zero invalidations while falsely reporting that all
  changes were saved; customer truth stayed on the old order.
- **Before/after operation estimate:** the reproduced pre-fix material reorder
  performed zero effective project writes, but that was data loss rather than a
  cost optimization. The corrected flow performs exactly the intended project
  update and its existing precise public-truth refresh. No duplicate write or
  duplicate analytics event was observed. The reversible Command Center
  hide/show lifecycle performed one intended write per acknowledged state; the
  customer preview converged after each. Generation defaults in the design
  surface remained a publish-draft boundary and wrote only when the global
  Publish action was explicitly invoked.
- This correction deliberately prioritizes canonical truth over a lower
  operation count. It adds no listener, polling, cache, Function fan-out,
  Storage operation, provider request, or background job. No Firebase cost
  reduction is claimed.

### Confirmed Firebase cost avoidance — MLRC-322 invalid External Sync activation

- **Original behavior:** mobile allowed an enabled draft with no provider URL
  to call Save. The save path first called the protected secret `ensure`
  transaction and could then persist an enabled/healthy store configuration
  with no delivery destination. Missing-secret state also offered Rotate, whose
  server path can create the first secret despite its destructive wording.
- **Original operation estimate:** each avoidable secret request reads the
  store, tenant, and secret documents and writes the server-owned secret plus
  the store version/status projection. The blank mobile Save could then issue
  the additional settings store update.
- **Change:** both clients disable rotation until a first secret exists; mobile
  keeps blank enabled drafts unsaveable and validates every enabled save;
  desktop validates and atomically persists its normalized URL before enabling.
- **After behavior:** the verified blank and unsafe mobile attempts performed
  zero secret requests, zero secret writes, zero store writes, and zero provider
  calls. This removes one three-read/two-write secret transaction plus the
  following settings write from each invalid blank activation attempt.
- **Correctness assessment:** valid HTTPS enablement, server-owned secret
  creation, selected-store authorization, explicit rotation, testing, and
  delivery remain unchanged. The correction reduces operations by preventing
  contradictory state rather than caching or weakening freshness.

### Confirmed Firebase/provider cost avoidance — MLRC-323 domain removal cancellation

- **Original behavior:** the reachable desktop Remove action immediately called
  the authorized custom-domain DELETE boundary. A mistaken click therefore ran
  the domain-removal transaction, provider cleanup attempt, and cache
  invalidation before the owner could recover.
- **Original operation estimate:** one accidental click produced the existing
  transaction's claim write plus store write, then one provider removal attempt
  and public-cache invalidation; pending provider cleanup can require the
  existing later claim-release work.
- **Change:** the first click now opens a named destructive confirmation. Only
  the explicit modal confirmation invokes the unchanged DELETE handler.
- **After behavior:** the browser-verified Cancel path produced zero Firestore
  writes, zero provider requests, and zero cache invalidations. Exact emulator
  readback retained the verified store domain and current store-owned claim.
- **Correctness assessment:** confirmed removal, authorization, claim release,
  provider cleanup, public freshness, error retry, and mobile behavior remain
  unchanged. The savings apply only to cancelled or mistaken actions and do not
  weaken a legitimate removal.

### Browser-delivery optimization — MLRC-270 printable ZIP compression

- **Original behavior:** one Complete Menu Kit download generated a 46,453,264-
  byte local ZIP; its three raster-backed PDFs accounted for 45,617,143 bytes.
- **Cause:** all five governed PDF render paths omitted jsPDF document-stream
  compression and embedded-PNG compression.
- **Change:** the existing renderers now use lossless PDF stream compression and
  jsPDF's `FAST` PNG compression while preserving file types, page dimensions,
  template output, and ZIP membership.
- **Before/after measurement:** 46,453,264 bytes before and 1,640,055 bytes
  after, a 96.47% reduction, with the same eleven archive entries and a clean
  archive-integrity test.
- **Firebase cost assessment:** this does **not** reduce a billed Firestore,
  Storage, or Function operation. The flow remains browser-local with zero
  Firebase operations. It reduces client memory, generated-file size, download
  time, and user-device storage; it is not represented as a Firebase billing
  optimization.
- **Evidence:** connected-Chrome downloads, exact byte and entry comparison,
  `unzip -t`, `pdfinfo`, the printable-asset verifier, adjacent print/export
  regressions, strict TypeScript, focused lint, and inventory evidence.

  the call-to-action bounding box enters the live QR bounding box.

### Output Center Menu Kit and outlet QR assessment

- The two acknowledged Menu Kit generations and two outlet-scoped QR

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-272 Saved design idempotency

- **Original behavior:** one owner save intent interrupted by unavailable
  Storage produced two template index records after the owner closed, reopened,
  and retried. Each record also owned a document object and preview object.
- **Cause:** the component-local click guard did not survive editor remounts,
  while the parent generated a new template identity for every call and did
  not expose the pending mutation through its editor-header busy state.
- **Change:** overlapping saves for the same tenant/store/asset/family reuse one
  reserved identity until all calls settle; the editor remains open and reports
  the existing wait recovery instead of starting another DAL mutation.
- **Before/after operation count:** before, one interrupted intent produced two
  Firestore template transactions and four Storage uploads (two documents and
  two previews). After, the same failure/recovery sequence produced one
  Firestore index record/version and the expected two Storage objects. The
  second in-flight activation produced no additional DAL call.
- **Correctness:** the reservation is scoped to the authenticated tenant,
  selected store, asset, and template family; the edited document title is
  retained. UI deletion and direct emulator readback confirmed zero residual
  index records and zero residual objects.
- **MLRC-273 cost assessment:** dialog naming changes only mounted accessibility
  attributes and adds zero Firestore, Storage, Function, cache, or provider
  operations.
- **MLRC-274 cost assessment:** inspector labels and browser-local canvas actions
  add zero Firestore, Storage, Function, listener, cache, analytics, or provider
  operations. Duplicate/delete/alignment changes were discarded without Save.
- **MLRC-275 cost assessment:** removing false controls and renaming the existing
  drawer handoff changes only browser-local composition. Background mode and
  canvas-size checks add zero Firebase, provider, cache, or analytics operation.
- **MLRC-276 cost assessment:** before the fix the dead CTA performed zero
  operations but failed the required owner workflow. After the fix, a confirmed
  action uses the existing one-project publication mutation and existing precise
  cache invalidation/verification path; it adds no listener, polling loop,
  collection scan, analytics fan-out, or new cache. Cancel performs zero writes,
  and the synchronous in-flight guard prevents repeated taps from starting a
  second publication attempt. The reversible availability propagation check used
  exactly one existing project write per owner toggle and restored the fixture.
- **MLRC-277 cost assessment:** this prevents an unnecessary local App Check
  module/bootstrap attempt and its diagnostic for browser-standard loopback
  subdomains. It does not disable App Check on QA, production, custom customer
  domains, or any non-loopback host, and no billed cloud-operation reduction is
  claimed.
- **MLRC-278 cost assessment:** the shared pure timestamp normalizer replaces
  two failed render-time conversions and their diagnostics. It adds no Firebase
  read/write, Function, Storage, analytics, listener, cache, or provider work.
- **MLRC-279 cost assessment:** normal dashboard rendering remains unchanged.
  One acknowledged publication now causes one existing project-document read
  in each open dashboard tab for the exact matching tenant/store/project. This
  event-driven read replaces an owner-trust stale state without polling, focus
  listeners, collection scans, unrelated-scope reads, new writes, or persistent
  cache data. Same-tab and malformed/cross-scope events add zero reads.
- **MLRC-280 cost assessment:** native label associations replace three
  pointer-only UI handlers. The correction adds no Firestore read/write,
  listener, Function, Storage, cache, analytics, provider, or network work.
- **MLRC-281 and MLRC-282 cost assessment:** the controlled-empty picker and
  setup-content clearance change only browser-local selection/layout behavior.
  The reversible test invoked no translation, extraction, Firestore read/write,
  listener, Function, Storage, cache invalidation, analytics, provider, or
  network operation.
- **MLRC-283 and MLRC-284 cost assessment:** confirmation recovery and
  duplicate-name validation are browser-local state changes. Invalid/cancelled
  paths add zero Firestore reads/writes, listeners, Functions, Storage, cache
  invalidation, analytics, provider requests, or network operations.
- **MLRC-285 cost assessment:** the reset target correction preserves the
  existing single acknowledged project-update path and precise invalidation.
  It prevents a write to the wrong project but adds no Firestore read/write,
  listener, Function, Storage, analytics, provider request, or cache. Pending
  state also prevents repeat submission while the one mutation is in flight.
- **MLRC-286 cost assessment:** a rejected linked-master delete retains the
  existing transaction reads and performs zero writes. Stable response codes
  and the one-toast browser mapping add no Firebase operation; removing the
  second local toast is a UX correction, not a billed-operation reduction. The
  successful cleanup delete used the existing one project tombstone plus one
  summary update and existing precise public/screen invalidation boundary.
- **MLRC-287 cost assessment:** before the fix, each repeated local seed could
  retain dynamic master/outlet documents and summary entries, so later selector
  reads and linked-outlet scans grew with test history. The emulator-only seed
  now performs two bounded collection reads, refuses more than 400 discovered
  documents, and deletes stale fixture records in the same deterministic batch.
  This reduces subsequent local QA reads and eliminates false fixture state;
  no cloud Firebase saving is claimed because the command refuses non-emulator
  execution.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-175 Analytics clean-state writes

- **Original behavior:** opening Mobile Analytics Settings made Save and Reset
  immediately actionable although the visible and persisted values were equal.
  One owner click could enter `updateStore`, write the store document through
  the normal nested-update projection, and request public-cache revalidation
  while producing no truth change.
- **Cause:** `JSON.stringify` compared objects with different property insertion
  order instead of comparing the governed analytics fields.
- **Change:** `areAnalyticsDraftsEqual` compares the explicit canonical draft
  key list. Invalid GA4, Search Console, and Meta Pixel values are also rejected
  before `saveInFlight` and therefore before `updateStore`.
- **Before/after estimate:** clean initial Save click: one store-document write
  plus one public-cache revalidation request → zero backend operations because
  the action stays disabled. Each malformed non-empty identifier submission
  likewise falls from a reachable save attempt to zero backend operations.
- **Correctness:** real changes still use the existing tenant/store ownership,
  nested-difference, write-result, local-state, and public-cache paths. No cache,
  query, listener, collection contract, or freshness behavior changed.
- **Evidence:** connected Chrome proved clean → dirty → Reset → clean states and
  all three pre-write validation recoveries; the shared normalization, public-
  truth, accessibility, strict TypeScript, lint, inventory, and production-build
  gates pass.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-178 domain availability preflight

- **Original behavior:** any trimmed subdomain of at least three characters and
  any custom-domain input of at least four characters enabled a backend
  availability request, including spaces, punctuation, and URL schemes.
- **Change:** shared pure claim-syntax normalizers now gate both desktop and
  mobile controls and re-check inside their handlers.
- **Before/after estimate:** each malformed click falls from one protected API
  or DAL availability request (plus its server/provider admission work) to zero
  backend, Firebase, or provider operations.
- **Correctness:** valid candidates keep the existing authoritative server
  validation, reserved-name, tenant/store, ownership, claim, and provider
  boundaries. No availability result is cached or reused across inputs.
- **Evidence:** real Chrome reproduced both malformed actions as enabled and
  then disabled after correction; the full custom-domain claim/provider suite
  and focused lint pass.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-181 first-source outlet propagation

- **Original behavior:** every newly created master menu immediately called the
  outlet propagation path while its canonical `files` array was empty. The
  active Firestore rule requires an inherited outlet project to reference a
  master with exactly one source, so each eligible target transaction was
  guaranteed to fail after prerequisite reads.
- **Cause:** propagation was attached to empty project creation rather than the
  first successful canonical source save.
- **Change:** empty menu creation performs no propagation. The first successful
  master save that changes from zero sources to one invokes the existing
  deterministic, bounded propagation once. Linked projects and ordinary later
  saves do not invoke it.
- **Before/after estimate:** empty-menu create: one tenant read, source/store
  authority reads, and one failed transaction attempt per eligible outlet ->
  zero propagation operations. Ordinary later save: zero propagation work both
  before and after. First valid source save performs the existing intended
  propagation once.
- **Correctness:** the change aligns the client trigger with the already-active
  single-source Rules contract and retains target-store rechecks, deterministic
  retry identity, atomic project/summary writes, and per-target failure
  isolation. It adds no cache, collection, index, Function, rule, or deployment.
- **Evidence:** three hosted empty-menu creates reproduced the denial on the
  provider-free two-location fixture. Authenticated read-only Rules API
  inspection confirmed the single-source requirement. The project-propagation,
  multi-location, public-truth, focused ESLint, and strict TypeScript gates pass
  locally.

### Cost-neutral correctness corrections — MLRC-179, MLRC-180, MLRC-182, MLRC-183, MLRC-184, MLRC-185, MLRC-186, MLRC-187, MLRC-188, MLRC-189, MLRC-190, MLRC-191, MLRC-192, MLRC-193, MLRC-194, MLRC-195, MLRC-196, MLRC-197, MLRC-198, MLRC-199, MLRC-200, MLRC-201, MLRC-202, MLRC-203, MLRC-204, MLRC-205, MLRC-206, MLRC-207, MLRC-208, MLRC-209, MLRC-210, and MLRC-211

- MLRC-184 changes only interaction semantics around the existing guarded store

- MLRC-192 does not add a Firestore write or cache invalidation. It awaits the

  was queued behind an in-flight write. Before: a rapid explicit edit could
  display success while its existing write/invalidation remained pending.
  After: one acknowledged explicit edit still produces one project write and
  one coalesced per-store invalidation; an actual second draft produces its
  already-required second write/invalidation instead of being silently delayed.
- MLRC-193 applies the same acknowledged boundary to item Add/Delete and
  category Add/Update/Delete/Reorder. It adds no write, read, listener,
  Function, Storage operation, cache entry, or invalidation. One explicit
  mutation still produces the same one project write and one coalesced
  per-store invalidation. A second flush occurs only when a distinct newer
  draft was queued behind an in-flight write, preventing false success and the
  duplicate user retries that false success can provoke.
- MLRC-194 adds an input sensor to the already-mounted reorder context only.
  It adds no Firestore read/write, listener, Function, Storage, analytics,

  Function, Storage, provider, cache, or invalidation path was added. All
  lifecycle writes used disposable emulator projects and both were removed.
- MLRC-197 adds no steady-state Firestore read, write, listener, Function,

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-106 disabled scheduler work and finalized leases

- **Observed behavior:** QA reported about 5.2k Function invocations over seven days, matching the every-two-minute maintenance schedule's 5,040 theoretical weekly ticks. The deployed target also reports `ENABLE_MESSAGING_ONBOARDING=false`.
- **Original operation shape:** despite that runtime gate, `messaging_intake` was selected on all 720 daily ticks. Its no-op path performed one lease transaction read/write, one outcome transaction read plus two writes, and one redundant fallback-release read. The active `special_menu_lifecycle` task had the same redundant post-finalization read.
- **Cause:** the provider flag was checked inside the task after task selection, and `finally` always attempted release although `recordTaskOutcome` had already released the owned lease atomically.
- **Change:** disabled tasks are filtered before cadence/lease selection. A finalized outcome records that its lease is already released; fallback release runs only after incomplete finalization. Messaging cleanup/retention stays feature-independent, and special-menu transitions remain every two minutes.
- **Before/after estimate:** 2,880 reads/day and 2,160 writes/day removed; 20,160 reads and 15,120 writes/week; 86,400 reads and 64,800 writes per 30 days. Function invocations remain 5,040/week because the release-critical special-menu lifecycle still owns the two-minute SLA.
- **Correctness:** per-task leases, current-owner verification, stale-owner rejection, retry recovery, outcome state, special-menu public truth, and retention obligations are unchanged.
- **Evidence:** Functions TypeScript build passed; `verify:firebase-scale-cost-closeout` now passes 124 static checks plus both lease emulator suites. The maintenance emulator proves provider-disabled intake is omitted, special-menu lifecycle remains due, stale owners cannot finalize, and the current owner atomically settles/releases its lease.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-068 menu-link input preflight

- **Original behavior:** syntactically invalid, embedded-credential, and non-HTTP(S) menu-link inputs could enable Import link and consume one protected `/api/menu-link-imports` invocation plus one rate-limit attempt before the server rejected them.
- **Cause:** the desktop form checked only non-empty copy and permission; the authoritative server URL boundary was the first syntax/protocol check.
- **Change:** a pure client preflight now keeps the action disabled and renders specific inline recovery for empty, malformed, overlong, credential-bearing, or non-HTTP(S) inputs. Admitted links are normalized and have fragments removed before the request.
- **Before/after estimate:** invalid input: 1 protected server invocation + 1 rate-limit attempt → 0 backend operations. Valid input is unchanged.
- **Correctness:** server DNS/IP/redirect/content-size/type, tenant/store/project, permission, rate-limit, Storage, and job-creation checks remain authoritative. No new cache or persistence was introduced.
- **Evidence:** pure URL-boundary regression and 387/387 extraction source checks pass. Exact hosted `1ec71b4…` kept unsafe and credential-bearing inputs disabled with specific alerts, admitted a valid owned HTTPS link, and returned to disabled after clearing; the source contract returns invalid input before the request helper.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-134 public create-menu link preflight

- **Original behavior:** `javascript:` remained submittable on the public create-menu link form and consumed one `/api/public/create-menu` invocation before the authoritative server boundary rejected it.
- **Cause:** the public adapter checked only non-empty input plus import permission while the owner Projects adapter already used the shared syntax/protocol/credential boundary.
- **Change:** the public adapter now uses the same pure validator, strips fragments through its normalized URL, renders localized inline recovery, and disables submission until both URL and permission are valid.
- **Before/after estimate:** malformed/protocol-invalid/credential-bearing input: 1 server invocation and its route admission work → 0 backend, Firebase, Storage, or provider operations. Valid requests retain the same server-side SSRF and content validation.
- **Evidence:** 388/388 extraction source checks, pure URL-boundary tests, strict TypeScript, focused ESLint, and connected Chrome at 390×844 pass. The post-fix invalid interaction never entered its loading state.

### Confirmed optimization — MLRC-042 persisted Firebase Auth bootstrap

- **Original behavior:** A hard owner-route reload inspected `firebaseAuth.currentUser` before the Firebase SDK restored its persisted browser actor. In the observed stress pass, this produced 30 successful `/api/auth/set-claims` requests before the intended 30-per-15-minute limiter returned 429.
- **Original operation estimate per unnecessary reload:** one protected server invocation, a product-user email query returning up to two documents, one canonical store document read, plus Firebase Admin user/claims/token work.
- **Cause:** persisted Firebase Auth state restores asynchronously, so an early `currentUser === null` was treated as a fresh authentication handoff.
- **Change:** await the pinned SDK's `authStateReady()` settlement before inspecting the current actor and claims. Matching restored tenant/store claims now reuse the existing browser session; new OAuth actors, missing actors, store changes, and claim mismatches retain the protected set-claims path.
- **Expected after behavior:** a valid matching hard reload falls from one set-claims invocation and up to three Firestore document reads to zero. This does not weaken authentication, tenant/store matching, rate limiting, or failure recovery.
- **Evidence:** `verify:auth-security-failure-matrix`, `verify:menulist-api-tenant-safety`, strict TypeScript, focused ESLint, exact verified build `04a736f…`, three successful Billing reloads, direct `/users` recovery, and Vercel request logs showing zero post-build set-claims calls.

generic retry could otherwise cause.

Confirmed optimization MLRC-020: one Billing History activation performs one
bounded payment-ledger query (limit 50) and one bounded billing-document query
(limit 50). Before the fix, two rapid activations could start two complete
request sets, for up to 200 returned document reads. Desktop and mobile now use

request set, for up to 100 returned document reads. Store switching resets the
scope-specific mobile guard and remounts the desktop history state, preserving
tenant/store correctness. This does not cache results across stores and does
not weaken freshness or authorization.

Confirmed optimization MLRC-047: the internal batch-image worker previously
attempted one SAFE_MODE Firestore document read for every request before
checking the Cloud Tasks project header and shared secret. Unauthorized traffic
could therefore bill one read and hold a server invocation until the Admin read
timed out; two reproduced probes reached 30 seconds. Admission now happens
first, so an unauthorized request performs zero Firebase operations and the
direct regression returned `403` in 0.029 seconds. Admitted workers retain the
same one SAFE_MODE read and all existing fail-open, limiter, job, provider,
Storage, accounting, and mutation behavior.

Confirmed managed-runtime correction MLRC-077: an admitted guarded request
previously attempted SAFE_MODE through an uncredentialed default Firebase app,
failed with `firestore/invalid-credential`, logged fail-open noise, and then
continued. The helper now uses the existing product-scoped WIF Firestore
service. Exact hosted `e00f96f…` reached post-SAFE_MODE body validation and
returned the expected `400`, with no credential or SAFE_MODE error in the
deployment logs. The intended success path remains one bounded document read;
no billed-read reduction is claimed. The improvement removes one failed
credential attempt and its retry/support noise without weakening the fail-open
contract or adding a cache.
